def autocorr_flatten(a, n_iters, gamma):
    a = normalize clip.
    M = nextpow2(2n-1)
    A = rfft(a, M)
    for it:
       c = irfft(A*A, M)[:2n-1] (real)
       mean = 1/(2n-1)
       maxc = c.max()
       if maxc - mean < eps: break
       # target: reduce c above threshold
       thresh = mean + q * (maxc - mean) maybe q=0.2 => keep 80% excess
       c_target = c.copy()
       c_target[c > thresh] = thresh + (c-target? maybe linear soft) 
       Or c_target = c - eta*relu(c-thresh)
       # Need c_target average remains ~mean? If lower peaks, total sum decreases; but autocorr sum must be 1. If we lower peaks, the "lost" mass should be redistributed to lows? In autocorr shaping, can renormalize c_target to sum=1 (since sum conv = sum(a)^2=1). If c_target sum changed, scale? Scaling changes score. Better keep sum=1: C_target = c; reduce peaks, increase troughs by same amount maintaining nonnegative. E.g., excess=relu(c-thresh), total_e=excess.sum(); c_target = c - excess + total_e/(length of lows?) distributed? More robust: c_target = c - excess + add_ratio*relu(thresh-c) maybe to preserve sum. Or simple c_target = c - excess + total_e / len_lows * (c<thresh). This flattens while preserving mean.
       Cspec = rfft(c_target, M) (length M, c padded zeros to M? c length 2n-1, M >= 2n-1) real; Cspec should be >=0.
       Cspec = np.maximum(Cspec.real,0)
       phase = A / abs(A) (handle zero)
       A_mag = sqrt(Cspec)
       # Preserve DC? The sum of a corresponds A[0]=sqrt(M)*sum? Actually zero-padding DFT: A[0]=sum a. If we take sqrt Cspec, Cspec[0] = sum c_target =1, sqrt=1. But original A[0]=M? Wait rfft of length M: A[0]=sum a =1. Cspec[0]=sum c_target =1. Good.
       A_new = sqrt(Cspec) * phase
       # maybe shift to keep phase continuity at zero? okay.
       a_new = irfft(A_new, M)[:n]
       a_new = clip(a_new,0)
       if a_new.sum < eps: break
       a_new /= a_new.sum
       # optional sym? no
       record best exact
    return a

Pseudo:
def adam_softmax_polish(a_init, time_limit, betas=[10,30,100,300,1000,3000,10000], steps_per_beta maybe 2000, lr=0.01):
    n=len(a)
    a0=normalize(a_init)
    if a0.min()<1e-12: z = log(a0+1e-8); else z=log(a0)
    # To allow zero boundary, maybe set z for small to min - shift.
    best_a=a0.copy(); best_sc=exact(a0)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    for beta in betas:
      # maybe recenter z to keep sum softmax stable: z -= z.max()
      for step in range(max_steps):
         if time>limit: break
         z -= z.max()  # periodic
         a = softmax(z)
         score_lse, ga = lse_grad_norm(a,beta)
         gz = a*ga - a*np.sum(a*ga)
         t+=1
         m=0.9*m+0.1*gz; v=0.999*v+0.001*gz*gz
         mhat=m/(1-0.9**t); vhat=v/(1-0.999**t)
         z -= lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
         # Occasionally compute exact
         if step%25==0:
            a_exact=softmax(z); sc=exact(a_exact); if sc<best_sc: best=a; ...
            # If no improvement many, maybe lower lr? adaptive.
      # After beta, set z from best_a to avoid drift? Maybe z = log(best+eps) and continue.
    return best_a, best_score

Let's design exact coordinate descent with 1D minimization for final polish. This could improve a few decimals without full optimization. Need implement robustly.

Coordinate exact update:
Input a normalized sum=1, n. We choose a set of variable indices (contributors to top peaks). For each j, compute old y=a[j], S0=1. Need arrays q0 = current conv c (length 2n-1). For coordinate j:
C_k contribution linear coefficient = a[k-j] if k-j in [0,n-1], else 0, but careful double? For q_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. Term involving x_j after replacing y:
q_k_new = q0_k - old_contrib + new_contrib.
old_contrib = y * A_k + (if 2j==k) y^2? Let's derive: In q0, terms with index i=j: a_j a_{k-j} if k-j valid = y * a_{k-j}. Terms with i=k-j: a_{k-j} a_j if valid and k-j != j = another y*a_{k-j}. If k-j=j (2j=k), same term a_j^2 counted once? In convolution sum, when i=j, it appears once. If we use both i and i'=k-j, they are not separate in the sum over i. The expression "old_contrib = y=A_{k-j} if valid + (if k-j valid and k-j != j?) y?" Actually q_k = ∑_{i=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(k,n-1)} a_i a_{k-i}. This sum over i. For a fixed j, it appears when i=j (if k-j valid) gives a_{k-j}. It also appears when i=k-j, but that's a separate i value unless i=j. If i=k-j and k-j != j, then term a_{k-j} a_j, also counted. Since the sum over i includes both i values if distinct. New similarly: if 2j==k, x^2; else 2 x a_{k-j} if k-j valid.

def select_coordinates(a,c,T=200,active_r=10,tol=1e-4):

    contrib=np.zeros(n)

       i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(k,n-1)+1

Coordinate polish loop:
def coordinate_polish(a_init, max_time=300, sweeps=10, T=120, C=40, recompute_every=20):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init); c=fast_conv(a); best=a.copy(); best_score=exact_score(a); current=best_score
    accepted_count=0; no_improve=0
    while time < deadline and no_improve < some:
       top=select(...)
       improved_sweep=False
       for j in top:
          if time>deadline: break
          x_best,y_best = coordinate_scan(a,c,j,C)
          if x_best < current - 1e-12:
              accept...
              improved_sweep=True
              accepted_count++
              no_improve=0
              if accepted_count % 50==0: recompute c=fast_conv(a); current=2n*c.max(); if current<best...; ...
       if recompute after sweep: c=fast_conv(a); current=...; update best
       if not improved_sweep: no_improve++ else 0; maybe reduce T? break if no_improve>3
   return best
Need coordinate_scan returns best x and score. It can use c (current normalized) and a. It should not modify. Use m=2n-1.
Implementation coordinate_scan:
def coordinate_scan(a,c,j,n,candidates=None):
    y=a[j]; S0=1.0? We maintain normalized sum 1. If y<=0: y=0.
    if candidates is None generate based y.
    m=2*n-1
    start=j; stop=min(m, j+n)
    L=stop-start
    if L<=0: return y, np.inf
    off = np.arange(start, stop) - j  # 0..L-1
    vals = a[off]
    lin = 2.0*vals.copy()
    self_local = -1
    s = 2*j
    if start <= s < stop:
        self_local = s - start
        lin[self_local] = 0.0
    old = y*lin.copy()  # 2 y vals
    if self_local >=0:
        old[self_local] = y*y
    c_base = c.copy()
    c_base[start:stop] -= old
    base_max = float(c_base.max())
    S = 1.0 - y + cand
    valid = S > 1e-12
    if not any: return y, inf
    # q_slice candidates
    # To avoid big memory if C large, loop candidates? Vector ok.
    # q = slice_base[None,:] + cand[:,None]*lin[None,:]
    # if self: q[:,self] += cand^2
    # max_slice = max(q,axis=1)
    # score = 2n*max(max_slice, base_max)/S^2
    # Need c_base slice: c_base[start:stop]
    slice_base = c_base[start:stop]
    # If self: adjust.
    # Also for candidates where cand invalid, set score inf.
    # Potential negative c_base due to floating? It should be nonnegative; clip? If c_base has small negatives, base_max okay. q_slice may have negatives? no.
    q = slice_base[None,:] + cand[None?]*lin? Use cand[:,None]
    if self_local>=0: q[:, self_local] += cand*cand
    max_slice = q.max(axis=1)
    max_total = np.maximum(max_slice, base_max)
    score = inf array; score[valid] = 2n*max_total[valid]/(S[valid]*S[valid])
    idx = argmin(score); if score min inf: return y; else return cand[idx], score[idx]

def softmax_lse_objective(z, beta):
    a=softmax(z)
    score, g_a = lse_score_grad_norm(a,beta)
    g_z = a*g_a - a*sum(a*g_a)
    # For numerical, subtract mean? softmax gradient sum zero; g_z sum zero.
    return score, g_z

def active_subgradient_polish(a, time_limit, tol=1e-6, max_iter=...):
   x=normalize; best=...; v_zero? momentum? 
   x_prev=x; g_prev=None; dx_prev=None
   for it:
      c=fast_conv(x); maxc=c.max(); active=np.where(c >= maxc - max(1e-12, tol*maxc))[0]
      # If active too many, take top K? Uniform over active maybe.
      p=uniform on active length m
      cross = conv(rev_x,p)[n-1:2n-1] (FFT)
      g=2*cross - 2*cross.mean() (tangent)
      # BB step using previous: s=x-x_prev; y=g-g_prev
      # Standard BB for minimization: gamma = (s·s)/(s·y) if s·y>0 else 1/norm(g)
      # Since direction d=-g. step= gamma.
      new = x - step*g; clip normalize; if exact score improves accept else reduce step.
   This could converge to stationary point. Need p FFT each iteration; similar to LSE. Tolerance can be adaptively shrink (1e-4,1e-5,1e-6). Could be powerful and cheap. Let's add as "active peak polish". It directly minimizes exact max via subgradient descent. Combine with LSE/coordinate.

Reduced L-BFGS can jointly optimize selected coordinates while frozen others, then update a. We can repeat with reselected indices. This may be more effective than full L-BFGS and faster. Full L-BFGS can also after.

Let's design `reduced_lbfgs(a, n_vars=300, beta=2000, maxfun=20000)`:
   n=len(a); index selection:
     c=fast_conv(a); maxc=c.max(); active = top 5 peaks among c (or within tol). contrib score as before.
     top_contrib = argsort(-contrib_score)[:n_vars//2]
     top_a = argsort(-a)[:n_vars//4]
     bottom_a = argsort(a)[:n_vars//4]  # to allow introducing small peaks? maybe
     # Ensure coverage: if n_vars > len(top)... add evenly spaced
     idx = np.unique(np.concatenate(...))
     if len(idx) < n_vars: add evenly spaced indices up to n_vars.
   x0=a[idx].copy(); fixed=a.copy()
   def red_obj(v, beta): arr=fixed.copy(); vclip=np.clip(v,0,1000); arr[idx]=vclip; return lse_score_unnorm(arr,beta)
   res=minimize(red_obj, x0, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=[(0,1000)]*len(idx), options...)
   a_new=fix; eval exact.

Select reduced indices function:
def select_reduced_indices(a, K, active_r=8):
   n=len(a); c=fast_conv(a)
   # active top lags: choose top indices sorted by c. But if many near max, top may exclude. We'll use top 8.
   top_lags = np.argsort(c)[-active_r:]
   contrib = zeros(n)
   for k in top_lags:
      i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(k,n-1)+1
      idx=np.arange(i0,i1)
      contrib[i0:i1] += c[k] * 2*a[i0:i1] * a[k-idx]
   # rank contributors weighted by peak value
   top_contrib = argsort(-contrib)[:K*2//3] maybe
   top_a = argsort(-a)[:K//4]
   low_a = argsort(a)[:K//6]
   # even spacing: if K large, step=n// (K//6)
   idx = unique(concat)
   if len(idx)<K: add even indices
   return idx[:K]

Coordinate polish can be run with a stopping condition. It recomputes c after each sweep? Let's implement robustly:
def coordinate_polish(a_init, deadline, max_coords=200, candidate_count=32, recompute_interval=10):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init)
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    c=fast_conv(a)
    current=2*n*float(c.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_score=current
    accepted=0; stalls=0
    outer=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and stalls < 8:
        outer += 1
        idx = select_contrib_indices(a,c, max_coords)
        sweep_improved=False
        for j in idx:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
            y=a[j]
            # quick coordinate scan
            x_new, sc_new = coordinate_scan(a,c,j,n, candidate_count)
            if sc_new < current - 1e-13:
                S=1 - y + x_new
                if S <= 1e-12: continue
                # Update a: only replace j then scale all
                a = a / S
                a[j] = x_new / S  # Wait if we do a = a / S first, a[j] becomes y/S; need set x_new/S.
                # c = c / (S*S) before or after?
                c = c / (S*S)
                # If we scaled a then set? Let's do:
                # a_old = a.copy? scaling all: a *= 1/S; a[j]=x_new/S; c *= 1/S^2.
                # But c update must use old c before scaling.
                a *= 1.0/S
                a[j] = x_new / S
                c *= 1.0/(S*S)
                current = 2*n*float(c.max())
                # Due to scaling, current approx equals sc_new but recompute max.
                if current < best_score - 1e-14:
                    best_score=current; best=a.copy()
                sweep_improved=True
                accepted +=1
                if accepted % recompute_interval ==0:
                    c=fast_conv(a)
                    current=2*n*float(c.max())
                    if current < best_score: best_score=current; best=a.copy()
        if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
        # end of sweep: recompute to clear drift
        c=fast_conv(a)
        current=2*n*float(c.max())
        if current < best_score: ...
        if not sweep_improved:
            stalls +=1
        else:
            stalls=0
            # If no big improvements, maybe stop? We'll stop after no sweep or many.
    return best,best_score

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget_s-60
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # get parent
    parent=None
    try:
       if 'height_sequence_1' in globals():
          p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          if p.size>=8:
             parent=normalize_nonneg(p)
    except: pass
    # Build candidate starts
    starts=[] # (label,a)
    if parent is not None:
       starts.append(('parent',parent.copy()))
       rev=parent[::-1].copy(); starts.append(('parent_rev',normalize_nonneg(rev))) # same score maybe
       sym=(parent+parent[::-1])/2; starts.append(('sym',normalize_nonneg(sym)))
       # upsample lengths
       for n in candidate_lengths(len(parent)):
          starts.append((f'up{n}', upsample(parent,n)))
    else:
       # fallback initializations: constant, cosine, random, maybe W=1+0.5cos(pi) etc
       for n in [4096,8192,12288]:
          x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
          ones=0.5*ones
          cos=0.5+0.4*cos(2pi x)
          asym? W = 1+0.5*cos(pi*(2x-1))? Not sure; include several.
    Evaluate quick score for each start; keep top maybe 6 by score.
```

`normalize_nonneg(a)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(max(1,a.size))/max(1,a.size)
    a=a/s
    # If max too large? normalized sum 1 so max<=1 unless n=1
    return a
```
But if length zero? use ones(1).

`next_pow2(m)`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <=0: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```
Check m=1 -> (0).bit_length()=0 ->1. m=2 -> (1).bit_length()=1 ->2. Good.

`fast_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a=np.ascontiguousarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    m=2*n-1
    M=next_pow2(m)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,M)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,M)[:m]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```

`exact_score(a)`:
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    n=a.size
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12: return np.inf
    c=fast_conv(a)
    return float(2.0*n*c.max()/(S*S))
```
If a normalized, S=1. For final verification, call evaluate_sequence separately.

`cross_p_a(p,a)`:
```
def cross_p_a(p,a):
    n=a.size
    M2=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    RA=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1].copy(), M2)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, M2)  # p length 2n-1
    out=np.fft.irfft(RA*P, M2)
    return out[n-1:2*n-1]
```
If p length maybe not 2n-1? pass correct. For full objective, p length m. Good.

`lse_score_unnorm(x,beta)`:
```
def lse_score_unnorm(x,beta):
    n=x.size
    arr=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    S=float(arr.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
        return 1e30, np.zeros_like(arr)
    if beta <= 0:
       c=fast_conv(arr); S...; return exact_score, gradient? For LSE not.
    c=fast_conv(arr)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    # if min too small, exp underflow okay
    e=np.exp(scaled)
    sume=float(e.sum())
    if sume <=0 or not finite: e = np.ones_like(scaled); sume=m
    L=cmax+np.log(sume)/beta
    p=e/sume
    cross=cross_p_a(p,arr)
    g_raw=2.0*cross
    S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
    score=2.0*n*L/S2
    grad=(2.0*n)*(g_raw/S2 - 2.0*L*arr/S3)
    # finite checks
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad),grad,0.0)
    return float(score), grad
```
If beta very large and cmax huge? normalized. Good.

`lse_score_norm(x,beta)`:
```
def lse_score_norm(x,beta):
    n=x.size; S=float(x.sum()); if S<1e-12 return inf zeros
    arr=x/S # maybe assume
    c=fast_conv(arr); ... L=...; cross=cross_p_a(p,arr); grad=4*n*cross/allersscore=2*n*L
```
Use normalized arr to be safe.

`adam_simplex`:

```
def adam_simplex(a, deadline, betas=(30,100,300,1000,3000,10000)):
    a=normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(a,float))
    best=a.copy(); best_score=exact_score(a)
    n=a.size
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; lr=0.02; no_improve=0
    for beta in betas:
        # beta step budget: not fixed, use until deadline; if no improvement and low beta, continue
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            x=a # use current best? We should optimize current a, not always reset. But if drift worsens, reset to best.
            score_lse, g = lse_score_norm(x,beta)
            if not finite: break
            g = g - np.mean(g)
            gn=float(np.linalg.norm(g))
            if gn < 1e-15: break
            t += 1
            m = 0.9*m + 0.1*g
            v = 0.999*v + 0.001*g*g
            mhat=m/(1-0.9**t)
            vhat=v/(1-0.999**t)
            x = x - lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
            x = np.clip(x,0,1000)
            S=x.sum()
            if S < 1e-12: break
            x=x/S
            a=x
            if t % 200 == 0:
                sc=exact_score(a)
                if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score=sc; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
                    lr *= 1.0? maybe keep
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
                    if no_improve > 10:
                        # reset to best and lower lr
                        a=best.copy(); lr*=0.7; no_improve=0
                        if lr < 0.001: break
        # after beta, if best improved maybe
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
    return best,best_score
```

`select_contrib_indices(a,c,limit)`:
```
def select_contrib_indices(a,c,limit):
    n=a.size
    maxc=float(c.max())
    # active r = min(8, len(c)//10?)
    active_count = min(8, max(1,len(c)//100))
    active = np.argsort(c)[-active_count:]
    contrib = np.zeros(n)
    for k in active:
        i0=max(0, int(k)-n+1); i1=min(int(k), n-1)+1
        idx=np.arange(i0,i1)
        contrib[i0:i1] += c[int(k)] * (2.0*a[i0:i1]*a[int(k)-idx])
    top=np.argsort(-contrib)[:limit]
    # add top a
    top_a=np.argsort(-a)[:max(1, limit//4)]
    idx=np.unique(np.concatenate([top, top_a]))
    if len(idx) < limit:
       # add even spaced
       step=max(1, n//max(1, limit-len(idx)))
       for i in range(0,n,step): if len<limit add
    return idx[:limit]
```
Need handle if limit > n. `limit = min(limit,n)`.

`coordinate_scan` detailed:
```
def coordinate_scan(a,c,j,n,candidate_count=40):
    y=float(a[j])
    if y<0: y=0
    candidates = make_candidates(y,n)
    L = min(n, 2*n-1-j)
    if L <= 0: return y, np.inf
    self_loc = j if j < L else -1
    lin = 2.0 * a[:L].copy()
    if self_loc >= 0: lin[self_loc]=0.0
    old = y * lin
    if self_loc >=0: old[self_loc] = y*y
    c_base = c.copy()
    seg = c_base[j:j+L].copy()
    seg -= old
    # Clip tiny negative in segment? 
    seg = np.maximum(seg,0.0)
    c_base[j:j+L] = seg
    base_max = float(c_base.max())
    S = 1.0 - y + candidates
    valid = S > 1e-12
    if not np.any(valid): return y, np.inf
    q = seg[None,:] + candidates[:,None]*lin[None,:]
    if self_loc >=0:
        q[:, self_loc] += candidates*candidates
    # If any q negative, clip? For max, negative no issue. But due round, use np.maximum(q,0)
    # Could be memory; maybe if candidates*lin produce negative? lin nonneg, seg nonneg.
    max_q = q.max(axis=1)
    tot = np.maximum(max_q, base_max)
    score = np.full(candidates.shape, np.inf)
    score[valid] = 2.0*n*tot[valid]/(S[valid]*S[valid])
    # If all inf, fallback y
    i = int(np.argmin(score))
    if not np.isfinite(score[i]): return y, np.inf
    return float(candidates[i]), float(score[i])
```
One subtlety: `lin = 2*a[:L]` where a is normalized before update. For non-self coordinates, if `k-j` equals j? self handled. If `k-j` not j, a[off] remains old y? But if off equals j? That's self. Good.
Candidate x can be large; raw q may become huge. Score maybe. Clip candidates to [0, 1] perhaps. make_candidates returns <=1.
Potential bug: base_max includes segment values after clipping to 0; if true c_base at segment had tiny negative, clipping to 0 may overestimate if actual negative? c cannot be negative physically; roundoff. Fine.

def gradient_simplex(a, deadline, betas):
   x=normalize; best=x; best_score=exact
   x_prev=x; g_prev=None; dx_prev=None
   for beta in betas:
      # maybe warm start: reset momentum? no
      for it in range(max_iters):
          if time>deadline break
          sc, g = lse_score_norm(x,beta)
          g -= mean(g)
          gn=norm(g)
          if gn<1e-15 break
          if dx_prev is None or g_prev is None:
             step = 1.0 / (10.0*np.sqrt(n))? maybe 1e-3
          else:
             s=x-x_prev; ygrad=g-g_prev
             sy=float(s.dot(ygrad))
             ss=float(s.dot(s))
             if sy > 1e-16 and ss >1e-16: step=ss/sy
             else: step=1.0/(10*np.sqrt(n))
          step = min(step, 0.05/(gn? no))
          # backtracking: while exact(x - step*g) > sc + ... try step/2 up to 10

          if sc_trial < current_lse? exact current? For descent, compare exact current. We have exact current maybe. If improves, accept; else step/=2.
```

`run_lbfgs_reduced`:
```
def run_lbfgs_reduced(a, beta, n_vars, maxfun, maxiter):
    n=len(a)
    K=min(n_vars,n)
    fixed=normalize_nonneg(a).copy()
    if K <=1: return exact_score(fixed),fixed
    idx=select_reduced_indices(fixed,K)
    x0=fixed[idx].copy()
    bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*idx.size
    res=minimize(reduced_objective, x0, args=(fixed, idx, beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'maxls':20,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-10})
    xopt=np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
    new=fixed.copy(); new[idx]=xopt
    new=normalize_nonneg(new)
    return exact_score(new), new, res
```

def refine_pipeline(a, deadline):
    a = normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64))
    best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = exact_score(a)
    print label? We can print in propose not here? Could print here with length.
    now = time.monotonic()
    total = max(5.0, deadline - now)
    # coordinate 20%
    coord1_dead = now + 0.20*total
    if coord1_dead - time.monotonic() > 15:
        a2,s2 = coordinate_polish(a, coord1_dead, max_coords=max(80, min(256, len(a)//20)), candidate_count=48)
        if s2 < best_sc: best_sc,best_a=s2,a2
        a = best_a if s2 < exact_score(a)? Use best_a as current? For subsequent, use best_a.
    # reduced LBFGS phase 40%
    red_dead = now + 0.60*total
    if red_dead - time.monotonic() > 60:
        a_cur=best_a.copy()
        # choose betas according to remaining
        if red_dead - time.monotonic() > 300:
            betas=(300,1000,3000,10000)
        elif >120: betas=(500,2000)
        else: betas=(1000,)
        for beta in betas:
            if time.monotonic() > red_dead - 30: break
            nvars = min(512, max(128, len(a_cur)//20)) # for 8k =>400, 16k=>512
            # maxfun based on time: maybe 5000 + int((remaining/60)*1000)
            rem = red_dead - time.monotonic()
            maxfun = int(min(40000, max(3000, rem*60)))? 60 evals/sec? Not know. Use 10000.
            maxiter = int(min(1000, maxfun//10))
            sc, a_new, _ = run_lbfgs_reduced(a_cur, beta, nvars, maxfun, maxiter)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc=sc; best_a=a_new.copy()
            # optionally use a_cur = a_new if not worse? If reduced worsens in exact, maybe LSE decreased but exact worse; don't set? But could be in better basin. Use if sc < exact_score(a_cur) maybe. We'll set a_cur = a_new if sc < exact_score(a_cur) or res success? simpler if sc < exact_score(a_cur): a_cur=a_new else a_cur=best_a
            a_cur = a_new if sc < exact_score(a_cur) else best_a.copy()
            # short coordinate after each reduced if time
            if red_dead - time.monotonic() > 20:
                d2 = time.monotonic() + min(20, red_dead-time.monotonic())
                a3,s3=coordinate_polish(a_cur, d2, max_coords=100)
                if s3<best_sc: best_sc=s3; best_a=a3.copy()
                a_cur=best_a.copy()
    # coordinate second phase 15%
    coord2_dead = now + 0.75*total
    if coord2_dead - time.monotonic() > 30:
        a2,s2=coordinate_polish(best_a, coord2_dead, max_coords=150)
        if s2<best_sc: best_sc=s2; best_a=a2
    # final: full LBFGS or reduced high beta + final coordinate
    final_dead = deadline
    a_cur=best_a.copy()
    if final_dead - time.monotonic() > 300 and len(a_cur) <= 12288:
        d_full = final_dead - 60
        sc,a_new,_=run_lbfgs_full(a_cur, 3000 or 8000, maxfun=20000, maxiter=3000)
        if sc<best_sc: ...
        a_cur=best_a
    elif final_dead - time.monotonic() > 120:
        # reduced high beta several
        for beta in [5000,20000]: ...
    # final coordinate until deadline-2
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 10:
        a2,s2=coordinate_polish(best_a, deadline-2, max_coords=200)
        if s2<best_sc: ...
    return best_a,best_sc
```

    # sort by first element using simple sort (Bubble? Many small lists)
    lst_sorted=[]
    for item in lst:
       pos=0
       while pos < len(lst_sorted) and lst_sorted[pos][0] < item[0]: pos +=1
       lst_sorted.insert(pos,item)
    return lst_sorted

def top_k_lags(c,k):
    if c.size <= k: return np.argsort(c)[-k:]
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    return idx[np.argsort(c[idx])]  # maybe not need sorted
```

def top_k_reverse(arr,k):
   k=min(k,arr.size); if k<=0 return np.array([],int); idx=np.argpartition(arr,-k)[-k:] if k<arr.size else np.arange(arr.size); return idx
```

Define `sort_by_score(items)`:
```
def sort_by_score(items):
    res=[]
    for item in items:
        val=item[0]
        lo=0; hi=len(res)
        while lo<hi:
            mid=(lo+hi)//2
            if res[mid][0] < val: lo=mid+1
            else: hi=mid
        res.insert(lo,item)
    return res
```
Use for lists small.

Thus safe objective:
```
def objsafe(arr,beta):
   S=sum arr
   a=arr/S
   score,g = lse_score_norm(a,beta)
   G = float(np.dot(a,g))
   grad = (g - a*G)/S
   return score, grad
```
For full x, grad wrt x. For reduced v, this grad full; return grad[idx]. This accounts for scale normalization. Does it match unnormalized formula? Modify `lse_score_norm`:
```
def lse_score_norm(a,beta):
    a=np.asarray(a); S= sum; if S<eps return inf zeros_regardless
    aa=a/S
    c=fast_conv(aa); cmax; L; p; cross; score=2n L; grad=4n cross
    return score, grad
```

`full_objective`:
```
def full_objective(x,beta):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    S=float(np.sum(x))
    if S<1e-12: return 1e30, np.zeros_like(x)
    a=x/S
    score, g = lse_score_norm(a,beta)
    G=float(np.dot(a,g))
    grad=(g - a*G)/S
    # finite
    return score, grad
```
If x has negative small due to L-BFGS? Bounds nonnegative, but if clip: `x=np.maximum(x,0.0)`. If some zero, S ok. Using max could make kink but min okay.

`reduced_objective(v,a_fixed, idx,beta)` similar:
```
arr = a_fixed.copy()
arr[idx] = np.maximum(v,0.0) # v bounds nonneg
S=sum(arr)
a=arr/S
score,g = lse_score_norm(a,beta)
G=dot(a,g)
grad_full=(g - a*G)/S
return score, grad_full[idx]
```

Let's code coordinate_polish carefully:
```
def coordinate_polish(a, deadline, max_coords=200, max_sweeps=200):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n=a.size; if n==0 return
    c=fast_conv(a)
    current = 2.0*float(c.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_score=current
    accepted=0; stalls=0; sweep=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and sweep < max_sweeps and stalls < 4:
        sweep += 1
        idx = select_contrib_indices(a,c,max_coords)
        improved=False
        for j in idx:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline or accepted >= 500? maybe break: break
            old_a = a.copy(); old_c = c.copy(); old_current = current
            y = a[j]
            x_new, sc_new = coordinate_scan(a,c,j,n)
            S = 1.0 - y + x_new
            if S <= 1e-12 or not finite: continue
            if sc_new + 1e-14 < old_current:
                a = old_a.copy()
                a[j] = x_new
                a = a / S
                c = fast_conv(a)
                current = 2.0*n*float(c.max())
                if current < old_current - 1e-14:
                    improved=True
                    accepted += 1
                    if current < best_score:
                        best_score=current; best=a.copy()
                else:
                    a=old_a; c=old_c; current=old_current
            # maybe if many coords accepted, reselect? We'll reselect each sweep.
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        # end of sweep: reselect next loop
        if not improved:
            stalls += 1
        else:
            stalls = 0
        # optional: if accepted == 0 break after two no improvements
    return best, best_score
```
Copying old_a/old_c for every coordinate scan may be heavy (copy 16k+32k for 200 coords =50MB per sweep) okay but time. We can avoid copy unless scan says improvement and S valid. First compute scan; if not improve, no copy. If improve candidate, then copy. Good.
```
sc_new = coordinate_scan(...)
if sc_new < old_current - eps:
   old_a=a.copy(); old_c=c.copy()
   update/new
   if actual current improves accept else revert
```

Let's detail `lse_score_norm`:
```
def lse_score_norm(a,beta):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
       return 1e30, np.zeros(n)
    aa=a/S
    if beta <= 1.0:
       # Polynomial smoothing? if beta small, LSE approx mean; use beta=1 maybe.
    c=fast_conv(aa)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    e=np.exp(scaled)
    se=float(e.sum())
    if se <= 0 or not np.isfinite(se):
       e=np.ones_like(scaled); se=float(e.size)
    L=cmax+np.log(se)/beta
    p=e/se
    cross=cross_p_a(p, aa)
    score=2.0*n*L
    grad=4.0*n*cross
    # finite
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
       grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad),grad,0.0)
    return float(score), grad
```
`cross_p_a` uses p length 2n-1; if n=1, M2 next_pow2(1) =1? `3*n-2=1`, okay. `out[n-1:2*n-1]` for n=1 -> out[0:1]. Good.
If beta extremely small, exp(scaled) ~1, L=mean; grad constant vector? cross = mean? It's okay. We pass beta>1.

`reduced_objective`:
```
def reduced_objective(v,a_fixed,idx,beta):
    arr=a_fixed.copy()
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64)
    arr[idx]=np.maximum(v,0.0)
    S=float(arr.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
       return 1e30, np.zeros_like(v)
    a=arr/S
    score,g=lse_score_norm(a,beta)
    G=float(np.dot(a,g))
    grad_full=(g - G) / S
    gv=grad_full[idx]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gv)): gv=np.where(np.isfinite(gv),gv,0.0)
    return float(score), gv
```
If idx length 0? Not.

`run_lbfgs_reduced`:
```
def run_lbfgs_reduced(a,beta,K,maxfun,maxiter):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a)
    n=a.size
    K=int(min(K,n))
    if K <=0: return exact_score(a),a,True
    fixed=a.copy()
    idx=select_reduced_indices(fixed,K)
    x0=fixed[idx].copy()
    bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*idx.size
    # if idx.size ==1, simple 1D? still minimize okay but overhead. Could scan coordinate? L-BFGS okay.
    opts={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'maxls':20,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-10,'maxcor':20}
    res=minimize(reduced_objective,x0,args=(fixed,idx,beta),method='L-BFGS-B',jac=True,bounds=bounds,options=opts)
    xopt=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64)
    if xopt.size != idx.size or not np.all(np.isfinite(xopt)): xopt=x0
    new=fixed.copy(); new[idx]=np.clip(xopt,0,1000)
    new=normalize_nonneg(new)
    sc=exact_score(new)
    return sc,new, bool(res.success)
```
Need `maxcor` option maybe accepted. Good.

`select_reduced_indices`:
```
def select_reduced_indices(a,K):
    n=a.size; K=min(int(K),n)
    c=fast_conv(a)
    base=select_contrib_indices(a,c, max(1, int(2*K//3)))
    top_a=np.argsort(-a)[:max(1,K//4)]
    low_a=np.argsort(a)[:max(1,K//5)]
    idx=np.unique(np.concatenate([base, top_a, low_a]))
    if idx.size < K:
        need=K-idx.size
        step=max(1,n//need)
        even=np.arange(0,n,step)
        idx=np.unique(np.concatenate([idx, even]))
    return idx[:K]
```
`np.argsort(a)` full sort O(n log n), okay. Could use partition but fine.
If K small. `base` may already K? We pass 2K/3; unique might exceed K.

`top_k_reverse`:
```
def top_k_reverse(arr,k):
    n=arr.size; k=int(min(k,n))
    if k <=0: return np.empty(0,dtype=int)
    if k >= n: return np.arange(n)
    idx=np.argpartition(arr, -k)[-k:]
    return idx
```
No need sorted.

`select_contrib_indices`:
```
def select_contrib_indices(a,c,limit):
    n=a.size; limit=int(min(limit,n))
    if limit <=0: return np.empty(0,int)
    maxc=1.0
    active=top_k_reverse(c, max(3, min(12, max(1,c.size//100))))
    contrib=np.zeros(n)
    for kk in active:
        k=int(kk)
        w=float(c[k])
        if w <=0: continue
        i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(k,n-1)+1
        idx=np.arange(i0,i1)
        contrib[i0:i1] += w*(2.0*a[i0:i1]*a[k-idx])
    top=top_k_reverse(contrib, max(1,int(limit*3//4)))
    top_a=np.argsort(-a)[:max(1, limit//5)]
    idx=np.unique(np.concatenate([top, top_a]))
    if idx.size < limit:
       # add even
       need=limit-idx.size
       step=max(1,n//need)
       idx=np.unique(np.concatenate([idx,np.arange(0,n,step)][:limit]))
    return idx[:limit]

def make_candidates(y,n):
    y=float(y)
    if not np.isfinite(y) or y<0: y=0.0
    base=max(y, 1.0/float(n))
    vals=np.array([0.0, y, 1.0/n, 2.0/n, 5.0/n, 10.0/n, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0], dtype=float)
    if base > 0:
        vals=np.concatenate([vals, base*_CAND_RATIOS])
    vals=np.clip(vals,0.0,10.0)
    vals=np.unique(vals)
    if vals.size > 80:
        # reduce by keeping endpoints and log spaced?
        # Use indices
        idx=np.linspace(0, vals.size-1, 64).astype(int)
        vals=vals[idx]
    vals=np.unique(np.concatenate([vals, np.array([0.0, min(y,10.0)])]))
    return vals
```

`upsample`:
```
def upsample(a,n):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=int(n)
    if a.size == n: return normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a.size == 1:
        return normalize_nonneg(np.full(n, a.item()))
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
    y=np.maximum(y,0.0)
    # possibly smooth with small box? skip
    return normalize_nonneg(y)
```
Interpolation may have values >1? normalized later.

def refine_pipeline(a, deadline):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=exact_score(a)
    n=len(a)
    start=time.monotonic()
    total=max(5.0, deadline-start)
    def? no nested. We'll code inline.
    # Phase 1 coordinate
    d1=start+0.25*total
    if d1-start>10:
       a1,s1=coordinate_polish(best, d1, max_coords=min(200,max(64,n//40)))
       if s1<best_score: best_score=s1; best=a1
       print(f"  coord1 {best_score:.8f} t")
    # Phase 2 reduced L-BFGS
    d2=start+0.75*total
    a_cur=best.copy()
    if d2-time.monotonic()>30:
       nvars=max(128,min(640,n//12))
       # budgets for betas
       rem_for_reduced=d2-time.monotonic()
       if rem_for_reduced>600:
          betas=[300,1000,3000,10000,30000]
       elif rem_for_reduced>250:
          betas=[500,2000,8000]
       elif rem_for_reduced>100:
          betas=[1000,5000]
       else:
          betas=[2000]
       for beta in betas:
          if time.monotonic() > d2 - 20: break
          rem=d2-time.monotonic()
          # allocate portion: max(5, rem/ (remaining betas index+1))? Simpler: use up to rem*0.7? But next beta gets rest.
          # To avoid one beta consuming all, set tentative_dead = time + min(rem*0.6, 400)
          bump_dead = time.monotonic() + min(rem*0.6, 400.0)
          maxfun = int(max(2000, min(25000, (bump_dead-time.monotonic())*25)))
          maxiter = int(max(100, min(2000, maxfun//5)))
          sc,a_new,ok=run_lbfgs_reduced(a_cur,beta,nvars,maxfun,maxiter)
          if sc < best_score - 1e-15:
              best_score=sc; best=a_new.copy(); print
              a_cur=best.copy()
          else:
              # if L-BFGS improved exact over a_cur? use a_cur=a_new if sc < exact_score(a_cur)
              sc_cur=exact_score(a_cur)
              if sc < sc_cur:
                  a_cur=a_new.copy()
              else:
                  a_cur=best.copy()
          # short coordinate if time
          if d2-time.monotonic()>15:
              a3,s3=coordinate_polish(a_cur, time.monotonic()+min(20.0, d2-time.monotonic()), max_coords=80)
              if s3<best_score: best_score=s3; best=a3.copy(); a_cur=best.copy()
    # Phase 3 final coordinate
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 10:
       a2,s2=coordinate_polish(best, deadline, max_coords=min(300,max(100,n//30)))
       if s2<best_score: best_score=s2; best=a2
    return best,best_score
```

```
def refine_pipeline(a, deadline):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    n = len(a)
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    if t_start >= deadline:
        return best_a,best_sc
    total = max(5.0, deadline - t_start)
    # phase1
    d_phase1 = t_start + 0.25*total
    if d_phase1 - t_start > 10.0:
       a1,s1=coordinate_polish(best_a, d_phase1, max_coords=max(64, min(220, n//25)))
       if s1 < best_sc:
          best_sc=s1; best_a=a1.copy()
       print(...)
    # phase2
    d_phase2 = t_start + 0.75*total
    a_cur=best_a.copy()
    if d_phase2 - time.monotonic() > 30.0:
       K = int(max(128, min(640, n//12)))
       rem_phase = d_phase2 - time.monotonic()
       if rem_phase > 700.0:
          betas = (300,1000,3000,10000,30000)
       elif rem_phase > 300.0:
          betas = (500,2000,8000)
       elif rem_phase > 120.0:
          betas = (1000,5000)
       else:
          betas = (2000,)
       for beta in betas:
           if time.monotonic() > d_phase2 - 15.0: break
           rem = d_phase2 - time.monotonic()
           # give each L-BFGS at most 250s
           max_phase_run = min(rem, 250.0)
           maxfun = int(max(2500, min(35000, max_phase_run * 35.0)))
           maxiter = int(max(150, min(2500, maxfun // 4)))
           sc, a_new, ok = run_lbfgs_reduced(a_cur, beta, K, maxfun, maxiter)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-15:
               best_sc=sc; best_a=a_new.copy(); a_cur=best_a.copy()
               print
           else:
               cur_sc=exact_score(a_cur)
               if sc < cur_sc:
                   a_cur=a_new.copy()
               else:
                   a_cur=best_a.copy()
           if d_phase2 - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
              coord_dead = time.monotonic() + min(25.0, d_phase2 - time.monotonic())
              a3,s3=coordinate_polish(a_cur, coord_dead, max_coords=80)
              if s3 < best_sc - 1e-15:
                  best_sc=s3; best_a=a3.copy(); a_cur=best_a.copy()
    # final coordinate
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 10.0:
       a2,s2=coordinate_polish(best_a, deadline, max_coords=max(100, min(350, n//20)))
       if s2 < best_sc:
          best_sc=s2; best_a=a2.copy()
    return best_a,best_sc

def sort_by_score(items):
    res=[]
    for item in items:
        val=float(item[0])
        lo=0; hi=len(res)
        while lo<hi:
            mid=(lo+hi)//2
            if float(res[mid][0]) < val:
                lo=mid+1
            else:
                hi=mid
        res.insert(lo,item)
    return res

```python
"""
Sequence optimizer for minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))**2
over non-negative sequences.

Main ideas:
- Work with scale-safe LSE-smoothed objectives and a normalization-safe gradient.
- Exploit the provided parent sequence if present, plus interpolated upsamples.
- Use a strong exact single-coordinate descent:
  for one coordinate j, the new objective as a function of x=a[j] can be
  evaluated exactly in O(n log n) candidate evaluations, including
  renormalization.
- Use reduced-variable L-BFGS-B on top peak-contributing coordinates plus
  high/low amplitude coordinates, with several LSE temperature values.
- Alternate coordinate polish and reduced L-BFGS under strict time budgets.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

# Precomputed logarithmic ratios for exact coordinate candidates.
_CAND_RATIOS = np.exp(np.linspace(-5.0, 5.0, 32))


def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    a = a / s
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    return a


def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    M = next_pow2(m)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, M)[:m]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def exact_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (S * S))


def cross_p_a(p, a):
    """Cross-correlation needed for dL/da when p has length 2n-1."""
    n = a.size
    M2 = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    RA = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1].copy(), M2)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, M2)
    out = np.fft.irfft(RA * P, M2)
    return out[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def lse_score_norm(a, beta):
    """
    Score and raw gradient for normalized sum-1 a:
        F(a) = 2*n*L, L=logsumexp(beta*(c-cmax))/beta + cmax.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf"), np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return 1e30, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    aa = a / S
    if beta <= 0.0:
        beta = 1.0
    c = fast_conv(aa)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    scaled = beta * (c - cmax)
    e = np.exp(scaled)
    se = float(e.sum())
    if se <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(se):
        e = np.ones_like(scaled)
        se = float(e.size)
    L = cmax + np.log(se) / beta
    p = e / se
    cross = cross_p_a(p, aa)
    score = 2.0 * n * L
    grad = 4.0 * n * cross
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return float(score), grad


def reduced_objective(v, a_fixed, idx, beta):
    """
    LSE objective for variable coordinates v, with other coordinates fixed.
    Gradient is computed for the normalized representation a=arr/sum(arr).
    """
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=-1000.0)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)

    arr = a_fixed.copy()
    if idx.size == 0:
        arr = v
    else:
        arr[idx] = v

    S = float(arr.sum())
    if S < 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return 1e30, np.zeros_like(v, dtype=np.float64)

    a = arr / S
    score, g = lse_score_norm(a, beta)

    # Chain rule through the normalization a = arr / S.
    G = float(np.dot(a, g))
    grad_full = (g - G) / S

    gv = grad_full if idx.size == 0 else grad_full[idx]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gv)):
        gv = np.where(np.isfinite(gv), gv, 0.0)
    return float(score), gv


def top_k_reverse(arr, k):
    arr = np.asarray(arr)
    n = arr.size
    k = int(min(k, n))
    if k <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    if k >= n:
        return np.arange(n, dtype=int)
    return np.argpartition(arr, -k)[-k:]


def select_contrib_indices(a, c, limit):
    """Select coordinates that most contribute to current loud peaks."""
    n = a.size
    limit = int(min(limit, n))
    if limit <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)

    active_count = max(4, min(16, len(c) // 1000))
    active = top_k_reverse(c, active_count)

    contrib = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for kk in active:
        k = int(kk)
        w = float(c[k])
        if w <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(w):
            continue
        i0 = max(0, k - n + 1)
        i1 = min(k, n - 1) + 1
        if i1 <= i0:
            continue
        idx = np.arange(i0, i1)
        contrib[i0:i1] += w * (2.0 * a[i0:i1] * a[k - idx])

    top_contrib = top_k_reverse(contrib, max(1, int(2 * limit // 3)))
    top_amp = np.argsort(-a)[:max(1, limit // 5)]

    idx = np.unique(np.concatenate([top_contrib, top_amp]))
    if idx.size < limit:
        need = limit - idx.size
        step = max(1, n // need)
        idx = np.unique(np.concatenate([idx, np.arange(0, n, step)]))
    return idx[:limit]


def select_reduced_indices(a, K):
    """Choose a reduced set of variables for L-BFGS."""
    n = a.size
    K = int(min(K, n))
    if K <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)

    c = fast_conv(a)
    contrib_idx = select_contrib_indices(a, c, max(1, int(2 * K // 3)))
    top_amp = np.argsort(-a)[:max(1, K // 4)]
    low_amp = np.argsort(a)[:max(1, K // 5)]

    idx = np.unique(np.concatenate([contrib_idx, top_amp, low_amp]))
    if idx.size < K:
        need = K - idx.size
        step = max(1, n // need)
        idx = np.unique(np.concatenate([idx, np.arange(0, n, step)]))
    return idx[:K]


def make_candidates(y, n):
    """Candidate raw values for one coordinate during exact coordinate descent."""
    y = float(y)
    if not np.isfinite(y) or y < 0.0:
        y = 0.0
    base = max(y, 1.0 / float(n))

    vals = np.array(
        [0.0, y, 1.0 / n, 2.0 / n, 5.0 / n, 10.0 / n,
         0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0],
        dtype=np.float64,
    )
    if base > 0.0:
        vals = np.concatenate([vals, base * _CAND_RATIOS])

    vals = np.clip(vals, 0.0, 10.0)
    vals = np.unique(vals)
    if vals.size > 80:
        idxs = np.linspace(0, vals.size - 1, 64).astype(int)
        vals = vals[idxs]

    vals = np.unique(np.concatenate([vals, np.array([0.0, min(y, 10.0)])]))
    return vals


def coordinate_scan(a, c, j, n):
    """
    Exact 1D optimization of coordinate j, allowing renormalization.

    Returns (x_new, predicted_normalized_score).
    """
    y = float(a[j])
    if not np.isfinite(y) or y < 0.0:
        y = 0.0

    L = min(n, 2 * n - 1 - j)
    if L <= 0:
        return y, np.inf

    cand = make_candidates(y, n)
    if cand.size == 0:
        return y, np.inf

    self_loc = j if j < L else -1
    lin = 2.0 * a[:L].copy()
    if self_loc >= 0:
        lin[self_loc] = 0.0

    old = y * lin
    if self_loc >= 0:
        old[self_loc] = y * y

    c_base = c.copy()
    seg = c_base[j:j + L].copy()
    seg -= old
    seg = np.maximum(seg, 0.0)
    c_base[j:j + L] = seg

    base_max = float(np.max(c_base))
    S = 1.0 - y + cand
    valid = (S > 1e-12) & np.isfinite(S)
    if not np.any(valid):
        return y, np.inf

    q = seg[None, :] + cand[:, None] * lin[None, :]
    if self_loc >= 0:
        q[:, self_loc] += cand * cand

    # q is nonnegative in exact arithmetic; safeguard tiny roundoff.
    if q.size:
        max_q = np.maximum.reduce(q, axis=1)
    else:
        max_q = np.zeros(cand.size, dtype=np.float64)

    total = np.maximum(max_q, base_max)
    score = np.full(cand.size, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    score[valid] = 2.0 * n * total[valid] / (S[valid] * S[valid])
    score = np.where(np.isfinite(score), score, np.inf)

    if np.all(np.isinf(score)):
        return y, np.inf

    i = int(np.argmin(score))
    return float(cand[i]), float(score[i])


def coordinate_polish(a, deadline, max_coords=200):
    """
    Strong local exact coordinate descent.
    Always keeps the best exact score found.
    """
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    c = fast_conv(a)
    current = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(c))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = current

    accepted = 0
    stalls = 0
    sweep = 0
    max_sweeps = 5000

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and sweep < max_sweeps and stalls < 4:
        sweep += 1
        idx = select_contrib_indices(a, c, max_coords)
        if idx.size == 0:
            break

        improved = False
        for jj in idx:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            j = int(jj)
            y = float(a[j])
            x_new, sc_new = coordinate_scan(a, c, j, n)
            S_new = 1.0 - y + x_new
            if S_new <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S_new):
                continue
            if sc_new >= current - 1e-14:
                continue

            old_a = a.copy()
            old_c = c.copy()
            old_current = current

            a = old_a.copy()
            a[j] = x_new
            a = a / S_new
            c = fast_conv(a)
            current = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(c))

            if current < old_current - 1e-14:
                improved = True
                accepted += 1
                if current < best_score:
                    best_score = current
                    best_a = a.copy()
            else:
                a = old_a
                c = old_c
                current = old_current

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        if best_score < current - 1e-15:
            a = best_a.copy()
            c = fast_conv(a)
            current = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(c))

        if improved:
            stalls = 0
        else:
            stalls += 1

    return best_a, best_score


def upsample(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == n:
        return normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a.size == 1:
        return normalize_nonneg(np.full(n, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64))

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    return normalize_nonneg(y)


def run_lbfgs_reduced(a, beta, K, maxfun, maxiter):
    """Reduced-variable L-BFGS-B on a smooth LSE surrogate."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    K = int(min(K, n))
    if K <= 0:
        return exact_score(a), a, True

    fixed = a.copy()
    idx = select_reduced_indices(fixed, K)
    if idx.size == 0:
        return exact_score(a), a, True

    x0 = fixed[idx].copy()
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * idx.size
    opts = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "maxfun": int(maxfun),
        "maxls": 20,
        "maxcor": 20,
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "gtol": 1e-10,
    }

    try:
        res = minimize(
            reduced_objective,
            x0,
            args=(fixed, idx, float(beta)),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options=opts,
        )
    except Exception:
        return exact_score(a), a, False

    xopt = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if xopt.size != idx.size or not np.all(np.isfinite(xopt)):
        xopt = x0
    xopt = np.clip(xopt, 0.0, 1000.0)

    new_a = fixed.copy()
    new_a[idx] = xopt
    new_a = normalize_nonneg(new_a)
    sc = exact_score(new_a)
    ok = bool(getattr(res, "success", False))
    return sc, new_a, ok


def sort_by_score(items):
    res = []
    for item in items:
        val = float(item[0])
        lo = 0
        hi = len(res)
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if float(res[mid][0]) < val:
                lo = mid + 1
            else:
                hi = mid
        res.insert(lo, item)
    return res


def refine_pipeline(a, deadline):
    """
    Allocate the time up to `deadline` between:
      1. exact coordinate polish,
      2. several reduced-variable L-BFGS refinements at increasing LSE beta,
      3. final exact coordinate polish.
    """
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    n = a.size
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    if t0 >= deadline:
        return best_a, best_sc

    total = max(5.0, deadline - t0)

    # Phase 1: coordinate polish.
    d1 = t0 + 0.25 * total
    if d1 - t0 > 10.0:
        try:
            mc = max(64, min(220, n // 25))
            a1, s1 = coordinate_polish(best_a, d1, max_coords=mc)
            if s1 < best_sc:
                best_sc = float(s1)
                best_a = a1.copy()
            print(f"  refine coord1 n={n} score={best_sc:.10f} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)
        except Exception:
            print("  refine coord1 failed", flush=True)

    # Phase 2: reduced L-BFGS.
    d2 = t0 + 0.75 * total
    a_cur = best_a.copy()
    if d2 - time.monotonic() > 30.0:
        K = int(max(128, min(640, n // 12)))
        rem_phase = d2 - time.monotonic()
        if rem_phase > 700.0:
            betas = (300, 1000, 3000, 10000, 30000)
        elif rem_phase > 300.0:
            betas = (500, 2000, 8000)
        elif rem_phase > 120.0:
            betas = (1000, 5000)
        else:
            betas = (2000,)

        for beta in betas:
            if time.monotonic() > d2 - 15.0:
                break
            rem = d2 - time.monotonic()
            run_time = max(5.0, min(rem, 260.0))
            maxfun = int(max(2500, min(35000, run_time * 35.0)))
            maxiter = int(max(150, min(2500, maxfun // 4)))

            try:
                sc, a_new, ok = run_lbfgs_reduced(a_cur, beta, K, maxfun, maxiter)
            except Exception:
                sc = np.inf
                a_new = a_cur
                ok = False

            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-15:
                best_sc = float(sc)
                best_a = a_new.copy()
                a_cur = best_a.copy()
                print(
                    f"  refine LBFGS beta={beta} n={n} "
                    f"score={best_sc:.10f} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s",
                    flush=True,
                )
            else:
                cur_sc = exact_score(a_cur)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_sc:
                    a_cur = a_new.copy()
                else:
                    a_cur = best_a.copy()

            if d2 - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
                cd = time.monotonic() + min(25.0, d2 - time.monotonic())
                try:
                    a3, s3 = coordinate_polish(a_cur, cd, max_coords=80)
                    if s3 < best_sc - 1e-15:
                        best_sc = float(s3)
                        best_a = a3.copy()
                        a_cur = best_a.copy()
                        print(
                            f"  refine post-LBFGS coord beta={beta} n={n} "
                            f"score={best_sc:.10f} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s",
                            flush=True,
                        )
                except Exception:
                    pass

    # Phase 3: final coordinate polish.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 10.0:
        try:
            mc = max(100, min(350, n // 20))
            a2, s2 = coordinate_polish(best_a, deadline, max_coords=mc)
            if s2 < best_sc:
                best_sc = float(s2)
                best_a = a2.copy()
            print(f"  refine final coord n={n} score={best_sc:.10f} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)
        except Exception:
            print("  refine final coord failed", flush=True)

    return best_a, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Returns a Python list of non-negative floats.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    usable = max(120.0, float(budget_s) - 60.0)
    global_deadline = t_start + usable
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    max_n = 16384

    # Try to use the known parent construction.
    parent = None
    try:
        if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
            p = np.asarray(globals()["height_sequence_1"], dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if p.size >= 8:
                p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
                parent = normalize_nonneg(p)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    starts = []

    if parent is not None:
        base = parent
        n0 = base.size

        # Symmetric average, capped to max_n.
        if n0 <= 200000:
            sym = 0.5 * (base + base[::-1])
            sym = normalize_nonneg(sym)
            if sym.size > max_n:
                sym = upsample(sym, max_n)
            starts.append(("sym", sym))

        lengths = set()
        if n0 <= max_n:
            lengths.add(n0)
        else:
            lengths.add(max_n)
        for nn in (4096, 8192, 12288, 16384):
            if nn >= 512 and nn <= max_n:
                lengths.add(nn)

        for nn in sorted(lengths):
            if nn == base.size:
                a = normalize_nonneg(base)
            else:
                a = upsample(base, nn)
            if a.size > max_n:
                a = upsample(a, max_n)
            starts.append((f"len{a.size}", a))
    else:
        # Fallback: several cheap parametric starts.
        for n in (4096, 8192, 12288):
            x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
            a1 = 0.5 * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            a2 = 0.5 + 0.35 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
            a3 = 0.5 + 0.30 * np.cos(np.pi * (2.0 * x - 1.0))
            a4 = 0.4 + 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x) ** 2
            a5 = np.clip(0.5 + 0.25 * rng.standard_normal(n), 0.01, 1000.0)
            starts.append(("const", normalize_nonneg(a1)))
            starts.append(("cos", normalize_nonneg(a2)))
            starts.append(("sympeak", normalize_nonneg(a3)))
            starts.append(("cos2", normalize_nonneg(a4)))
            starts.append(("rand", normalize_nonneg(a5)))

    scored = []
    for label, a in starts:
        if time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 120.0:
            break
        if a.size > max_n:
            a = upsample(a, max_n)
        a = normalize_nonneg(a)
        sc = exact_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((float(sc), label, a))

    scored = sort_by_score(scored)

    if not scored:
        best_a = normalize_nonneg(np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64))
        best_sc = exact_score(best_a)
    else:
        top_starts = scored[:min(5, len(scored))]
        best_a = top_starts[0][2].copy()
        best_sc = float(top_starts[0][0])

        # Quick screening round for several promising starts.
        quick_total = 0.18 * usable
        weights = [0.34, 0.26, 0.20, 0.12, 0.08]
        active_weights = weights[:len(top_starts)]
        if not active_weights:
            active_weights = [1.0]
        ws = float(sum(active_weights))

        quick_results = []
        for i, (sc0, label, a) in enumerate(top_starts):
            if time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 120.0:
                break
            frac = active_weights[i] / ws
            qd = time.monotonic() + max(20.0, quick_total * frac)
            print(
                f"Quick {label} n={a.size} initial={sc0:.10f} "
                f"deadline in {qd-time.monotonic():.1f}s",
                flush=True,
            )
            try:
                a1, s1 = refine_pipeline(a, qd)
                quick_results.append((float(s1), label, a1.copy()))
                if s1 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = float(s1)
                    best_a = a1.copy()
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Quick {label} failed: {e}", flush=True)

        quick_results = sort_by_score(quick_results)
        if quick_results:
            if quick_results[0][0] < best_sc:
                best_sc = float(quick_results[0][0])
                best_a = quick_results[0][2].copy()
            top3 = quick_results[:3]
        else:
            top3 = [(best_sc, "main", best_a)]

        # Final deeper refinement on the best surviving candidates.
        rem_start = time.monotonic()
        rem_total = max(0.0, global_deadline - rem_start)
        weights2 = [0.50, 0.30, 0.20]
        active2 = weights2[:len(top3)]
        if not active2:
            active2 = [1.0]
        ws2 = float(sum(active2))
        cum = 0.0

        for i, (sc0, label, a) in enumerate(top3):
            if rem_total <= 30.0 or time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 20.0:
                break
            frac = active2[i] / ws2
            fd = rem_start + rem_total * (cum + frac)
            cum += frac
            fd = min(fd, global_deadline - 5.0)
            if fd - time.monotonic() < 20.0:
                continue

            print(
                f"Deep {label} n={a.size} score_so_far={sc0:.10f} "
                f"deadline in {fd-time.monotonic():.1f}s",
                flush=True,
            )
            try:
                a1, s1 = refine_pipeline(a, fd)
                if s1 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = float(s1)
                    best_a = a1.copy()
                    print(f"  NEW BEST: {best_sc:.10f} n={a1.size}", flush=True)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Deep {label} failed: {e}", flush=True)

    # Ensure final length is safe and valid.
    if best_a is None or best_a.size == 0:
        best_a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    if best_a.size > max_n:
        best_a = upsample(best_a, max_n)
    best_a = normalize_nonneg(best_a)

    out = []
    s = 0.0
    for val in best_a:
        try:
            fv = float(val)
        except Exception:
            fv = 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(fv):
            fv = 0.0
        if fv < 0.0:
            fv = 0.0
        if fv > 1000.0:
            fv = 1000.0
        out.append(fv)
        s += fv

    if len(out) == 0 or s < 0.01:
        out = [0.5] * 1024

    # Final validation if the evaluator is available in the namespace.
    verified = best_sc
    eval_fn = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
    if callable(eval_fn):
        try:
            verified = float(eval_fn(out))
        except Exception:
            verified = float(best_sc)

    print("=" * 70, flush=True)
    print(f"FINAL internal={float(best_sc):.10f} verified={float(verified):.10f}", flush=True)
    print(f"Length={len(out)} elapsed={time.monotonic()-t_start:.1f}s", flush=True)
    print("=" * 70, flush=True)

    return out
```
